\displaystyle\left\{\left\lVert\mathsf{dum}_{i}\right\rVert_{{}_{\mathsf{op}}}^{b}=\left\lVert\mathsf{dum}_{i}^{b}\right\rVert_{{}_{\mathsf{op}}}\leqslant\frac{1+o_{n}(1)}{\delta n}\right\}\,.

The
\left\lVert\fE_{i}[v_{i}v_{i}^{\mathsf{T}}]\right\rVert_{{}_{\mathsf{op}}}\leqslant\frac{1+o_{n}(1)}{A(\varepsilon)n}
constraint

This constraint uses the matrix Sum-of-Squares framework (
Section

4.1
) to encode the constraint on the operator norm of the conditional pseudo-variance matrix of every iterate variable
v_{i}
. The constraint translates to the following two constraints,

\fE_{i}[v_{i}v_{i}^{\mathsf{T}}]\preceq\frac{1+o_{n}(1)}{A(\varepsilon)n}\Id\,,\;\;-\fE_{i}[v_{i}v_{i}^{\mathsf{T}}]\preceq\frac{1+o_{n}(1)}{A(\varepsilon)n}\Id

which is encoded using slack matrices
S_{i,1}
and
S_{i,2}

\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}
as,

\fE_{i}[v_{i}v_{i}^{\mathsf{T}}]=\frac{1+o_{n}(1)}{A(\varepsilon)n}\Id-S_{i,1}S_{i,1}^{\mathsf{T}}\,,

and,

\fE_{i}[v_{i}v_{i}^{\mathsf{T}}]=S_{i,2}S_{i,2}^{\mathsf{T}}-\frac{1+o_{n}(1)}{A(\varepsilon)n}\Id\,.

The “approximate-LDP” constraint

We now give the encoding of the constraint that the conditional covariance is close in Frobenius norm to a low-degree polynomial in the Wiener-Hermite basis.

\left\{\left\lVert\left(\fE_{i}\left[v_{i}v_{i}^{\mathsf{T}}\right]^{>d^{\prime}}\right)^{\otimes d}\right\rVert^{2}_{F}\leqslant\frac{o_{n}(1)}{n^{d}}\right\}

is interpreted as, for all
j\in[i-1]
and all
d^{\prime\prime}\in[d]
,

\left\{\fE_{j}\left\lVert\left(\left[\fE_{i}\left[v_{i}v_{i}^{\mathsf{T}}\right]-\sumop\displaylimits_{\alpha:\;|\alpha|>(d^{\prime})^{i-j}}\mathscr{W}_{j}\left[\fE_{j+1}\fE_{i}\left[v_{i}v_{i}^{\mathsf{T}}\right]\right](\alpha)\,\mathsf{He}_{\alpha}\left(v_{j}\mid\fE_{j}v_{j}v_{j}^{\mathsf{T}}\right)\right]\right)^{\otimes d^{\prime\prime}}\right\rVert^{2}_{F}\leqslant\frac{o_{n}(1)}{n^{d^{\prime\prime}}}\right\},

using the definitions in
Section

3.5.2
and
Section

4.2.7
.

E.2
Sum-of-squares proofs for Hermite-Weiner polynomials via cumulants

It is not hard to verify that the cumulant constraints presereve the orthogonality of the Hermites and the (weak) orthogonality of the Hermite-Weiner expansion.
It is quite straightforward to see by the recurrence relation defintion of the Hermites that their expectation is a valid moment in the polynomial grammar, and that it continues to be
0
.

Proposition E.1

(SoS zero-expectation for Hermites)
.

Given the cumulant constraints in
Equation

P.3
, it is the case that,

\E[\mathsf{He}_{n}(x_{1},\dots,x_{n})]=\begin{cases}1&\text{if }n=0\,,\\
0&\text{if }n\geqslant 1\,.\end{cases}

Proof.

The proof is a consequence of
[
Ter21
, Theorem 4.1]
with the choice of
m=1
and the observation that any closed diagram of this type cannot exist. First, note that
\E[\mathsf{He}_{n}(x_{1},\dots,x_{n})]
is an element of the polynomial grammar under the right choice of filtration. The fact that this is a SoS proof follows as the derivation of
[
Ter21
, Theorem 4.1]
does not invoke the density function of the gaussian, and the moments of a single gaussian can be counted as the number of perfect matchings. Therefore, by the equivalence of cumulants and moments via the Bell polynomials, the proof is a sum-of-squares proof in the variables of the polynomial grammar.
∎

One can substitute
x_{1},\dots,x_{n}
to be indices over any multi-set
\alpha
with
|\alpha|\leqslant d
and
\alpha_{i}\in\{v_{i1},\dots,v_{in}\}
for
i\in[k]
and evaluate the Hermites conditioned on prior iterates
v_{1},\dots,v_{i-1}
to apply the above lemma to the formal polynomials in the grammar of the grammar of the SDP.

A simple proposition similar to
Proposition

E.1
is that Isserlis’ lemma can be invoked in a sum-of-squares proof under the HES constraints.

Proposition E.2

(SoS Isserlis Lemma)
.

Given the constraints of
Equation

P.3
, for any degree sequence
\alpha=(\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{n})
with
|\alpha|\leqslant d
it is the case that,

\E\left[\prodop\displaylimits_{i\in\alpha}x_{i}^{\alpha_{i}}\right]=\sumop\displaylimits_{\pi\in P_{2}\{i_{1},\dots,i_{k}\},\alpha_{i_{j}}\neq 0}\prodop\displaylimits_{(a,b)\in\pi}\kappa_{{2}}\left[{x_{a}x_{b}}\right]\,.

Proof.

The proof follows by using the equivalence between moments and cumulants using the Bell polynomials and then using that defintition to invoke the moment-cumulant formula with the constraints in
Equation

P.3
.

\displaystyle\E\left[\prodop\displaylimits_{i\in\alpha}x_{i}^{\alpha_{i}}\right]

\displaystyle=_{\text{distinct indices}}\E[y_{1},\dots,y_{|\alpha|}]

\displaystyle=_{\text{moment-cumulant formula}}\sumop\displaylimits_{\pi\in\mathcal{L}[|\alpha|]}\prodop\displaylimits_{b\in\pi}\kappa_{{|b|}}\left[{x_{b_{1}},\dots,x_{b_{|b|}}}\right]

\displaystyle=_{\text{\lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{eqs:the-program-intro-cum}}}\sumop\displaylimits_{\pi\in P^{2}\{1,\dots,|\alpha|\}}\prodop\displaylimits_{(j,k)\in\pi}\kappa_{{2}}\left[{y_{j}y_{k}}\right]

\displaystyle=_{\text{substitute index map}}\sumop\displaylimits_{\pi\in P^{2}\{1,\dots,|\alpha|\}}\prodop\displaylimits_{(j,k)\in\pi}\kappa_{{2}}\left[{x_{\alpha(j)}x_{\alpha(k)}}\right]\,.

∎

Proposition E.3

(SoS orthogonality for Hermites)
.

Given the entropy constraints of the program,

\{\text{HES constraints}\}\mathrel{|}\joinrel\mkern-0.5mu\mathrel{-}_{d}\left\langle\mathsf{He}_{\alpha}(x),\mathsf{He}_{\beta}(x)\right\rangle=\fE_{1}[\mathsf{He}_{\alpha}(x)\mathsf{He}_{\beta}(x)]=\begin{cases}1&\text{if }\alpha=\beta\,,\\
0&\text{ otherwise}\,.\end{cases}

Proof.

The proof will directly invoke the evaluation of the product of the Hermite polynomials as given in
[
Ter21
, Proposition 4.1]
in terms of the underlying covariances. As one can derive from the recurrence relation definition of the Hermites in conjunction with the moment-cumulant formula, that the coefficients of the Hermites depend entirely on the underlying covariance matrix, the expectation of the product depends on the
products
of the covariances.

Set
m=2
with
b_{1}=(y_{1},\dots,y_{|\alpha|})
and
b_{2}=(y_{1},\dots,y_{|\beta|})
(using distinct indices) in
[
Ter21
, Proposition 4.1]
and note that,

\displaystyle\langle\mathsf{He}_{\alpha}(x),\mathsf{He}_{\beta}(x)\rangle=\E\left[\mathsf{He}_{\alpha}(x)\mathsf{He}_{\beta}(x)\right]

\displaystyle=\E\left[\mathsf{He}_{|\alpha|}(y_{1},\dots,y_{|\alpha|})\mathsf{He}_{|\beta|}(y_{1},\dots,y_{|\beta|})\right]=\delta_{|\alpha||\beta|}\,.

∎

A similar argument as the one above can be provided to demonstrate the weak orthogonality of the Hermite-Weiner polynomials
[
Rah17
, Definition 3]
, which is the orthogonal decomposition critically used for bounding the polynomials corresponding to the higher-order derivatives, as well as reasoning about “closeness” of moments and pseudo-moments in the rounding procedure.

Fact E.4

(SoS weak-orthogonality for Hermite-Weiner polynomials)
.

The HES SoS hierarchy provides the weak-orthogonality of Hermite-Weiner polynomials up to the requisite degree bound. More formally,

\{\text{HES constraints}\}\mathrel{|}\joinrel\mkern-0.5mu\mathrel{-}_{d}\fE_{1}\mathsf{He}_{\alpha}(v_{i})\mathsf{He}_{\beta}(v_{i})=\begin{cases}\sumop\displaylimits_{\pi\in S_{|\alpha|}}\prodop\displaylimits_{i=1}^{|\alpha|}\left(\fE_{i}[v_{i}v_{i}^{\mathsf{T}}|\mid v_{1},\dots,v_{i-1}]\right)_{i,\pi(i)}&\text{if }|\alpha|=|\beta|\,,\\
0&\text{ otherwise}\,,\end{cases}

for every
\alpha,\beta\in[n]
with
|\alpha|,|\beta|\leqslant d
.

The proof for this follows for similar reasons as
Proposition

E.3
with the use of
[
Ter21
, Proposition 4.3]
with the recursive definition of the Hermites as an Appell sequence. Since this is an equality and ultimately all terms become polynomials of the entries of the conditional covariance (and constants), the equality is a SoS proof over the grammar described in
Section

4.2.4
.
